But wait - in membrane proteins, aromatic residues (W, Y) and His are often found at the membrane interface (the &quot;aromatic belt&quot;). So a TM helix with W and H at its ends is actually very common and normal! The KD scale underestimates the membrane propensity of W and Y.

If I use a modified scale that better reflects membrane behavior, TM3 would likely pass.

Similarly, TM4 has issues with the KD scale because of the Y and S residues, but in reality, T and S can be in TMs (especially at helix ends), and Y is often at the membrane interface.

Given the biological reality of membrane proteins:
- TM helices often have aromatic residues (W, Y) and sometimes H at the interface
- The core of the helix should be hydrophobic
